Surah: 78. Follow the brief below (surah_map.md) exactly. The evidence is text.md (the surah), dictionary.md (every root of the surah's words, each branch with the classical dictionaries' own phrases), channels.md and hft.md (both are earlier readers' proposals: ignore their judgements (grades, strength or confidence labels, reading types, statements of what a reading may or may not do) and make your own) and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Return only the map as your final message.

===== _commentary/v16/prompts/r2/surah_map.md =====
Read the surah as one text and write its map of image chains: the lexical images that run through several of
its ayat and join them into one scene, process or movement. A later writer will read one ayah at a time, with
only that ayah's own dictionary. The map lets that writer hear what the ayah's words carry in the surah as a
whole, including senses whose evidence sits under the words of other ayat.

Your evidence is the surah text, the dictionary of every root in the surah (each branch with the classical
dictionaries' own phrases), earlier readers' channel review (channels.md) and activation hypotheses (hft.md),
and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Where a member or passage comes from memory rather than from
the dictionary or the text, say so. Do not use tools, delegate, browse or inspect files.

The channel review and the HFT records are proposals by earlier readers. Ignore their judgements: grades,
strength or confidence labels, reading types, words such as "surprising", "exploratory" or "latent", and every
statement of what a reading may or may not do. Make your own judgement from the surah's words, the dictionary
phrases and the Quran. Do not rediscover what they already assembled: start from their chains, test each one
against the dictionary phrases and the text, and join, extend, split or correct them. Where their wording
abstracts a member, go back to the dictionary phrase and name what it actually says.

A chain belongs on the map when its members are senses the dictionary attests for words that stand in the
surah, and together they make one image or process that the surah's wording or sequence lets a listener hear.
A member need not be the sense that translates its word in its own ayah; a chain is heard across the surah,
not in one word. Say plainly when a member is a rare sense or is attested only inside a fixed expression, and
keep it. When the dictionary itself joins two of the surah's words in one phrase, quote that phrase: it is
the strongest evidence a chain can have. Keep a scene at the level of its objects, their parts and their
operation. When proposals share members, do not fold one into another's more abstract function unless nothing
concrete is lost. Carry every chain that meets this test, however unusual; leave out proposals that do not.

Write the map in English, with Arabic quoted exactly (surah wording from the text, dictionary phrases from the
dictionary). It is working material for the writer, not commentary prose.

1. `## Chains`. For each chain, a `###` heading naming the image. One paragraph on what the image is and how it
   moves through the surah. Then its members, one line each: the ayah ref, the word as it stands in the text,
   the root and branch id, the dictionary's own phrase quoted exactly, and what this member contributes to the
   image. Add Quran passages outside the surah, with exact refs, where they stage or confirm the chain.
2. `## Ayat`. For each ayah in order: which chains it opens, advances or completes, and what its words add to
   each.
3. `## Not carried`. Each channel subchannel and each HFT record you did not carry into a chain, one line each:
   its name and why.

No ranking and no labels of strength or confidence. No list of what a writer must include. There is no length
target and no required number of chains or members.


===== _commentary/v16/work/s078/surah.r2/text.md =====
# Surah 78

- 78:1 عَمَّ يَتَسَآءَلُونَ
- 78:2 عَنِ ٱلنَّبَإِ ٱلْعَظِيمِ
- 78:3 ٱلَّذِى هُمْ فِيهِ مُخْتَلِفُونَ
- 78:4 كَلَّا سَيَعْلَمُونَ
- 78:5 ثُمَّ كَلَّا سَيَعْلَمُونَ
- 78:6 أَلَمْ نَجْعَلِ ٱلْأَرْضَ مِهَٰدًۭا
- 78:7 وَٱلْجِبَالَ أَوْتَادًۭا
- 78:8 وَخَلَقْنَٰكُمْ أَزْوَٰجًۭا
- 78:9 وَجَعَلْنَا نَوْمَكُمْ سُبَاتًۭا
- 78:10 وَجَعَلْنَا ٱلَّيْلَ لِبَاسًۭا
- 78:11 وَجَعَلْنَا ٱلنَّهَارَ مَعَاشًۭا
- 78:12 وَبَنَيْنَا فَوْقَكُمْ سَبْعًۭا شِدَادًۭا
- 78:13 وَجَعَلْنَا سِرَاجًۭا وَهَّاجًۭا
- 78:14 وَأَنزَلْنَا مِنَ ٱلْمُعْصِرَٰتِ مَآءًۭ ثَجَّاجًۭا
- 78:15 لِّنُخْرِجَ بِهِۦ حَبًّۭا وَنَبَاتًۭا
- 78:16 وَجَنَّٰتٍ أَلْفَافًا
- 78:17 إِنَّ يَوْمَ ٱلْفَصْلِ كَانَ مِيقَٰتًۭا
- 78:18 يَوْمَ يُنفَخُ فِى ٱلصُّورِ فَتَأْتُونَ أَفْوَاجًۭا
- 78:19 وَفُتِحَتِ ٱلسَّمَآءُ فَكَانَتْ أَبْوَٰبًۭا
- 78:20 وَسُيِّرَتِ ٱلْجِبَالُ فَكَانَتْ سَرَابًا
- 78:21 إِنَّ جَهَنَّمَ كَانَتْ مِرْصَادًۭا
- 78:22 لِّلطَّٰغِينَ مَـَٔابًۭا
- 78:23 لَّٰبِثِينَ فِيهَآ أَحْقَابًۭا
- 78:24 لَّا يَذُوقُونَ فِيهَا بَرْدًۭا وَلَا شَرَابًا
- 78:25 إِلَّا حَمِيمًۭا وَغَسَّاقًۭا
- 78:26 جَزَآءًۭ وِفَاقًا
- 78:27 إِنَّهُمْ كَانُوا۟ لَا يَرْجُونَ حِسَابًۭا
- 78:28 وَكَذَّبُوا۟ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا كِذَّابًۭا
- 78:29 وَكُلَّ شَىْءٍ أَحْصَيْنَٰهُ كِتَٰبًۭا
- 78:30 فَذُوقُوا۟ فَلَن نَّزِيدَكُمْ إِلَّا عَذَابًا
- 78:31 إِنَّ لِلْمُتَّقِينَ مَفَازًا
- 78:32 حَدَآئِقَ وَأَعْنَٰبًۭا
- 78:33 وَكَوَاعِبَ أَتْرَابًۭا
- 78:34 وَكَأْسًۭا دِهَاقًۭا
- 78:35 لَّا يَسْمَعُونَ فِيهَا لَغْوًۭا وَلَا كِذَّٰبًۭا
- 78:36 جَزَآءًۭ مِّن رَّبِّكَ عَطَآءً حِسَابًۭا
- 78:37 رَّبِّ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَمَا بَيْنَهُمَا ٱلرَّحْمَٰنِ ۖ لَا يَمْلِكُونَ مِنْهُ خِطَابًۭا
- 78:38 يَوْمَ يَقُومُ ٱلرُّوحُ وَٱلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةُ صَفًّۭا ۖ لَّا يَتَكَلَّمُونَ إِلَّا مَنْ أَذِنَ لَهُ ٱلرَّحْمَٰنُ وَقَالَ صَوَابًۭا
- 78:39 ذَٰلِكَ ٱلْيَوْمُ ٱلْحَقُّ ۖ فَمَن شَآءَ ٱتَّخَذَ إِلَىٰ رَبِّهِۦ مَـَٔابًا
- 78:40 إِنَّآ أَنذَرْنَٰكُمْ عَذَابًۭا قَرِيبًۭا يَوْمَ يَنظُرُ ٱلْمَرْءُ مَا قَدَّمَتْ يَدَاهُ وَيَقُولُ ٱلْكَافِرُ يَٰلَيْتَنِى كُنتُ تُرَٰبًۢا


===== _commentary/v16/work/s078/surah.r2/dictionary.md =====
# Dictionary: every branch of every focus root

Every root of the surah's words, each once, headed by the ayah and word where it first occurs; it also serves the later words listed with it.
One entry per branch: a Turkish label and Turkish glosses of its attested senses, then the classical dictionaries' own phrases with their source tags (ayn, sihah, tahdhib, maqayis, mufradat, jamhara). [kalıp] marks a sense the dictionaries attest only inside a fixed expression.
Identity roots come from the quran-data gateway. Word-scoped alternatives are cited analyses of this
exact word. **Echo roots** are observed but withheld mappings: sound-family candidates, not identity.

  فوقت الفصيل أي سقيته اللبن فواقا فواقا وتفوق الفصيل إذا شرب اللبن كذلك (sihah)؛ أتفوقه تفوق اللقوح أي أقرأ منه شيئا بعد شيء (sihah;tahdhib;maqayis)؛ المفوق الذي يؤخذ قليلا قليلا من مأكول أو مشروب (tahdhib)؛ فوق فصيلك أي اسقه ساعة بعد ساعة وظل يتفوق المخض (mufradat)؛ لا يستفيق من الشراب أي لا يجعل لشربه وقتا (tahdhib)؛ خرجنا بعد أفاويق من الليل أي بعدما تمضي عامة الليل (tahdhib)

- **B010** çiçek veya meyve kılıfı — üzüm salkımının veya hurma çiçeğinin kılıfı · hurma çiçeğinin ya da meyvenin kılıfı · hurma ağacından çıkan kapalı çiçek kılıfları
  الكافور كم العنب قبل أن ينور وسمى كافورا لأنه كفر الوليع أي غطاه (maqayis)؛ الكافور كم العنب قبل أن ينور وكافوره ورقة الذي يستره والكافور الطلع والكفرى والكوافير (ayn)؛ الكافور الطلع ووعاء طلع النخل وكذلك الكفرى (sihah)؛ الكافور اسم أكمام الثمرة التي تكفرها والكافور أكمام الثمرة (mufradat)
- **B011** koku maddesi, su kaynağı veya bitki — güzel kokulu karışımlarda kullanılan madde · cennetteki bir su kaynağı · çiçeği papatyaya benzeyen bir bitki
  الكافور شيء من أخلاط الطيب والكافور عين ماء في الجنة والكافور نبات نوره كنور الأقحوان (ayn)؛ الكافور من الطيب (sihah)؛ الكافور الذي هو من الطيب (mufradat)
- **B012** uzak arazi; köy, uzak yer halkı veya mezar — insanlardan uzak, pek uğranmayan arazi · köy veya mezar · köyler veya uzak yerlerin halkı
  الكفر من الأرض ما بعد من الناس وأهل الكفور والقرى (maqayis)؛ الكافر من الأرض ما بعد عن الناس والكفور القرى (ayn)؛ الكفر أيضا القرية والكفر أيضا القبر (sihah)؛ الكافر من الأرض ما بعد عن الناس (tahdhib)
- **B013** dağ geçidi; iri dağ veya alçak duvar — dağ geçitleri · dağ geçidi veya iri dağ · alçak duvar
  الكفرات والكفر الثنايا من الجبال (maqayis)؛ الكفر الثنايا من الجبال (ayn)؛ الكفر العظيم من الجبال (sihah)؛ الكافر الحائط الواطىء (tahdhib)
- **B014** eğilerek boyun eğme gösterisi — başını eğmek veya elini göğsüne koyup eğilmek
  التكفير إيماء الذمي برأسه لا يقال سجد له وإنما يقال كفر له (ayn)؛ التكفير أن يخضع الإنسان لغيره يضع يده على صدره ويتطامن له (sihah)
- **B015** hükümdara taç giydirme veya taç — hükümdara taç giydirme veya tacın kendisi
  التكفير تتويج الملك بتاج والتكفير ههنا التاج نفسه (ayn)

(source: latent_activation/network/v3/reviews/s078/reader_a_pilot.md)

# s078 Semantic Channel Discovery

## Parent Channels

### 1. P1 (78:1-16): Contested News Moving Toward Disclosure
- Semantic invariant: Information moves from reciprocal demand through transmission and marking toward disclosure, while counterfeit or obstructed speech interrupts that movement.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:1-5 anchors reciprocal questioning (`س ء ل`), the great report (`ن ب ء`), disagreement (`خ ل ف`), and eventual knowing (`ع ل م`).
- Surprising reach: The report is materialized as something requested, delivered, extracted from concealment, marked like a route, and vulnerable to both forgery and vocal blockage.

#### Subchannel A. Question, Requested Object, and Answered Need
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Interlocutors exchange questions, identify a sought object, and move toward satisfaction of the request through an arriving report and acquired knowledge.
- Active motifs: asking and requesting (`quranic:root_000661:B001/m01`); the sought need (`quranic:root_000661:B002/m01`); satisfying a request (`quranic:root_000661:B003/m01`); reciprocal questioning (`quranic:root_000661:B004/m01`); transmitted report (`quranic:root_001464:B002/m01`); disclosure to a knower (`quranic:root_001040:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:1 `س ء ل` (يَتَسَآءَلُونَ); 78:2 `ن ب ء` (ٱلنَّبَإِ); 78:4-5 `ع ل م` (سَيَعْلَمُونَ).
- Synthesis: The opening inquiry is not only talk about a topic: its lexical field supplies a requester, a requested object, reciprocal exchange, and fulfillment. The report then functions as the delivered answer whose content becomes uncovered knowledge.

#### Subchannel B. Announcement, Marker, and Extraction from Concealment
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An authorized announcer delivers information, a visible mark guides recognition, and hidden content is brought out and set in its proper place.
- Active motifs: divine announcer and prophetic transmission (`quranic:root_001464:B003/m01`); guiding mark or landmark (`quranic:root_001040:B002/m01`); bringing a hidden thing out (`quranic:root_000400:B002/m01`); delivered descent (`quranic:root_001492:B002/m01`); clear road over easy ground (`quranic:root_001464:B007/m01`); placing a thing in its fitting station (`quranic:root_001492:B004/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:2 `ن ب ء` (ٱلنَّبَإِ); 78:4-5 `ع ل م` (سَيَعْلَمُونَ); 78:14 `ن ز ل` (أَنزَلْنَا); 78:15 `خ ر ج` (لِّنُخْرِجَ).
- Synthesis: Announcement is rendered as guided emergence: what was inaccessible is delivered downward, extracted into view, and recognized by a mark that also behaves like a route indicator. Knowledge thus acquires spatial direction and an appointed placement.

#### Subchannel C. Counterfeit Claim, Broken Promise, and Degraded Account
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Rival accounts diverge, a promise fails to arrive, fabricated speech masquerades as creation, and a false claimant assumes prophetic authority.
- Active motifs: divergent paths and disputed accounts (`quranic:root_000433:B004/m01`); failed promise or missed appointment (`quranic:root_000433:B005/m01`); degraded successor or poor speech (`quranic:root_000433:B008/m01`); fabricated discourse (`quranic:root_000434:B007/m01`); false prophetic claim (`quranic:root_001464:B004/m01`); the liar called a lamp (`quranic:root_000693:B005/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:2 `ن ب ء` (ٱلنَّبَإِ); 78:3 `خ ل ف` (مُخْتَلِفُونَ); 78:8 `خ ل ق` (خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ); 78:13 `س ر ج` (سِرَاجًۭا).
- Synthesis: Disagreement opens into a full counterfeit-production scene: paths separate, promised correspondence fails, and an invented account can even claim revelatory status. Calling a liar a “lamp” sharply pressures the surface lamp image by distinguishing illumination from persuasive glare.

#### Subchannel D. Faint Report and Constricted Tongue
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A message ranges from forceful oratory to a barely perceptible sound, while dryness, wrapping, and blockage impede its vocal instrument.
- Active motifs: faint ambiguous sound (`quranic:root_001464:B006/m01`); forceful eloquent speaker (`quranic:root_000195:B003/m01`); thirst-constricted tongue (`quranic:root_001019:B014/m01`); wrapped, heavy tongue (`quranic:root_001365:B004/m01`); speech made difficult (`quranic:root_000217:B009/m01`); thickened tongue as bodily organ (`quranic:root_001029:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:2 `ن ب ء` (ٱلنَّبَإِ); 78:7 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالَ); 78:14 `ع ص ر` (ٱلْمُعْصِرَٰتِ), `ث ج ج` (ثَجَّاجًۭا); 78:16 `ل ف ف` (أَلْفَافًا).
- Synthesis: The report’s communicative force is tested at the level of sound production. Eloquent release stands opposite a tongue tightened by thirst, heaviness, or obstruction, while the report itself may arrive only as a faint, uncertain sound.

### 2. P1 (78:1-16): A Habitable Frame Prepared, Fastened, and Covered
- Semantic invariant: Habitable space is produced by preparing a lower surface, fixing structural supports, assembling an overhead frame, and adding a protective covering.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:6-12 names the earth as a bed, mountains as pegs, and seven strong structures built above, while 78:10 makes night a garment.
- Surprising reach: The cosmic arrangement repeatedly takes the scale of a cradle, tent, rib cage, woven shield, and fitted sleeping cloth.

#### Subchannel A. Leveled Ground as Bed, Rug, and Cradle
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A lower surface is softened, leveled, spread, and prepared to receive a resting body.
- Active motifs: lower earth opposite the sky (`quranic:root_000025:B001/m01`); thick woolen rug (`quranic:root_000025:B005/m01`); infant cradle (`quranic:root_001451:B001/m01`); spread and leveled bedding (`quranic:root_001451:B002/m01`); preparing and regularizing a matter (`quranic:root_001451:B003/m01`); smooth even surface (`quranic:root_000434:B008/m01`); softness and levelness (`quranic:root_000665:B008/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:6 `ء ر ض` (ٱلْأَرْضَ), `م ه د` (مِهَٰدًۭا); 78:8 `خ ل ق` (خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ); 78:9 `س ب ت` (سُبَاتًۭا).
- Synthesis: The earth-bed is a worked receiving surface rather than inert ground. Rug, cradle, smoothing, and preparation make its suitability an achieved condition, with sleep supplying the body for which that condition is made.

#### Subchannel B. Pegged Supports and Load-Bearing Frame
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A hard raised mass receives driven pegs and tightened joints, while ribs and posts distribute the structure’s load.
- Active motifs: raised solid mountain mass (`quranic:root_000217:B001/m01`); substrate too hard to excavate (`quranic:root_000217:B005/m01`); driven peg (`quranic:root_001620:B001/m01`); fixed upright like a peg (`quranic:root_001620:B003/m01`); ribs and house posts as supports (`quranic:root_000156:B009/m01`); tightened knot or fastening (`quranic:root_000782:B001/m01`); strength and solidity (`quranic:root_000782:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:7 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالَ), `و ت د` (أَوْتَادًۭا); 78:12 `ب ن ي` (بَنَيْنَا), `ش د د` (شِدَادًۭا).
- Synthesis: Mountain and peg become parts of one fastening mechanism. The same scene is bodily and architectural: ribs, posts, hard substrate, and secured joints explain how a heavy frame can stand and bear pressure.

#### Subchannel C. Assembled Overhead and Numbered Strength
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Components are joined into a composite structure located above, counted into seven units, and made resistant.
- Active motifs: joining parts into a building (`quranic:root_000156:B001/m01`); composite built configuration (`quranic:root_000156:B002/m01`); hide-built shelter or canopy (`quranic:root_000156:B004/m01`); upper direction (`quranic:root_001188:B001/m01`); sevenfold count and multiplication (`quranic:root_000669:B001/m01`); structural hardness (`quranic:root_000782:B002/m01`); making a thing into a specified state (`quranic:root_000248:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:6, 78:9-11, 78:13 `ج ع ل` (نَجْعَلِ/جَعَلْنَا); 78:12 `ب ن ي` (بَنَيْنَا), `ف و ق` (فَوْقَكُمْ), `س ب ع` (سَبْعًۭا), `ش د د` (شِدَادًۭا).
- Synthesis: Building, number, placement, and strength operate as one assembly sequence: parts are joined, raised, counted, and transformed into a resistant overhead. The hide canopy keeps the architecture materially close to a shelter.

#### Subchannel D. Protective Skin, Wrap, and Sleeping Cover
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A body or enclosure is hidden by clothing, armor, shield, wrap, and a dedicated sleeping textile.
- Active motifs: clothing as sensory cover (`quranic:root_001341:B001/m01`); armor worn for protection (`quranic:root_001341:B002/m01`); concealment from perception (`quranic:root_000266:B001/m01`); protective shield (`quranic:root_000266:B008/m01`); hiding place (`quranic:root_000266:B017/m01`); wrapping one layer around another (`quranic:root_001365:B001/m01`); sleeping cloth or place (`quranic:root_001568:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:9 `ن و م` (نَوْمَكُمْ); 78:10 `ل ي ل` (ٱلْيْلَ), `ل ب س` (لِبَاسًۭا); 78:16 `ج ن ن` (جَنَّٰتٍ), `ل ف ف` (أَلْفَافًا).
- Synthesis: Night’s garment extends into a layered defensive skin. Clothing, shield, hiding place, wrap, and sleeping textile all interpose a fitted material between a vulnerable interior and exposure.

### 3. P1 (78:1-16): Cessation, Recovery, and Alternating Time
- Semantic invariant: Activity is interrupted so that bodies and systems can rest, recover, and re-enter an alternating temporal cycle.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:9-11 moves from sleep and cessation through night-cover to daylight livelihood.
- Surprising reach: Sleep approaches death and burial, while wakefulness is figured as returning strength and stagnant systems are tested by whether circulation resumes.

#### Subchannel A. Sleep as Cut-Off Motion and Restored Capacity
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Motion is cut, sleep overcomes the body, and repose permits awareness and strength to return.
- Active motifs: ordinary sleep and recumbency (`quranic:root_001568:B001/m01`); sleep overcoming the person (`quranic:root_001568:B002/m01`); trusting repose in something (`quranic:root_001568:B005/m01`); stillness and rest (`quranic:root_000665:B001/m01`); cutting and removing activity (`quranic:root_000665:B002/m01`); recovery of awareness and strength (`quranic:root_001188:B005/m01`); prepared cradle (`quranic:root_001451:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:6 `م ه د` (مِهَٰدًۭا); 78:9 `ن و م` (نَوْمَكُمْ), `س ب ت` (سُبَاتًۭا); 78:12 `ف و ق` (فَوْقَكُمْ).
- Synthesis: Sabat acts mechanically as a cut in motion, not merely a pleasant state. The cut is reversible: the sleeper settles into trust, then returns to awareness and usable strength.

#### Subchannel B. Death-Like Stillness, Shroud, and Burial
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The sleeping body crosses toward death, is wrapped and hidden, and is opposed by the lexical field of continuing life.
- Active motifs: death expressed as sleep (`quranic:root_001568:B008/m01`); motionless illness, fainting, or death (`quranic:root_000665:B001/m02`); concealment of the dead (`quranic:root_000266:B009/m01`); wrapping a corpse in a shroud (`quranic:root_001365:B001/m02`); last breath rising from the chest (`quranic:root_001188:B010/m01`); life and condition of living (`quranic:root_001067:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:9 `ن و م` (نَوْمَكُمْ), `س ب ت` (سُبَاتًۭا); 78:11 `ع ي ش` (مَعَاشًۭا); 78:12 `ف و ق` (فَوْقَكُمْ); 78:16 `ج ن ن` (جَنَّٰتٍ), `ل ف ف` (أَلْفَافًا).
- Synthesis: The rest scene reaches its limiting case in a body whose movement does not resume. Shroud and concealment materialize that limit, while “living” in the daylight livelihood scene keeps reversible rest distinct from final cessation.

#### Subchannel C. Night-Day Succession and Measured Phase
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Darkness and daylight take one another’s place across named periods, with work, rest, and maturity assigned to phases.
- Active motifs: darkness of night (`quranic:root_001392:B001/m01`); acting or traveling by night (`quranic:root_001392:B002/m01`); the night adjoining a day (`quranic:root_001392:B003/m01`); daylight opening by illumination (`quranic:root_001559:B002/m01`); alternation and replacement (`quranic:root_000433:B006/m01`); recurring era or time (`quranic:root_001019:B001/m01`); a span of time (`quranic:root_000665:B006/m01`); day at its height (`quranic:root_000782:B005/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:3 `خ ل ف` (مُخْتَلِفُونَ); 78:9 `س ب ت` (سُبَاتًۭا); 78:10 `ل ي ل` (ٱلْيْلَ); 78:11 `ن ه ر` (ٱلنَّهَارَ); 78:12 `ش د د` (شِدَادًۭا); 78:14 `ع ص ر` (ٱلْمُعْصِرَٰتِ).
- Synthesis: The night-day pair is a replacement mechanism: one phase recedes as another arrives, and each bears a characteristic operation. Broader periods, the day’s height, and adjoining nights turn the alternation into a measured temporal architecture.

#### Subchannel D. Stagnation Tested by Renewed Circulation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Water, trade, cloth, and growth become still or depleted, then are tested by whether flow, market movement, or vegetation returns.
- Active motifs: standing water and general stillness (`quranic:root_001568:B009/m01`); market stagnation and worn cloth (`quranic:root_001568:B007/m01`); renewed plant or property after loss (`quranic:root_000433:B010/m01`); river flow cutting the earth (`quranic:root_001559:B001/m01`); returning milk between milkings (`quranic:root_001188:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:3 `خ ل ف` (مُخْتَلِفُونَ); 78:9 `ن و م` (نَوْمَكُمْ); 78:11 `ن ه ر` (ٱلنَّهَارَ); 78:12 `ف و ق` (فَوْقَكُمْ).
- Synthesis: Cessation becomes diagnostic when transferred from a sleeper to water, trade, cloth, and vegetation. The counter-image is renewed circulation: water cuts a course, milk returns, and growth replaces what had disappeared.

### 4. P1 (78:1-16): Rain Converted into Stored Flow, Growth, and Food
- Semantic invariant: Water is generated and released under pressure, routed into catchments, converted into plant growth, and finally incorporated as nourishment.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:14-16 explicitly descends abundant water, brings out grain and plants, and culminates in densely entwined gardens.
- Surprising reach: The rain sequence behaves like an engineered and metabolic supply chain, complete with pressure, conduits, reservoirs, extraction, swallowing, and bodily increase.

#### Subchannel A. Cloud Pressure, Release, and Downward Delivery
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Cloud or wind bears water, pressure releases it, and repeated descending pulses become a heavy pour.
- Active motifs: rain-bearing cloud or wind (`quranic:root_001019:B003/m01`); rotating dust-lifting storm (`quranic:root_001019:B004/m01`); forceful pouring and downflow (`quranic:root_000195:B001/m01`); descent from above (`quranic:root_001492:B001/m01`); causing water to descend (`quranic:root_001492:B002/m02`); repeated cloud-water pulses (`quranic:root_001188:B008/m01`); cloud emerging into view (`quranic:root_000400:B005/m01`); cloud named as nāhūr (`quranic:root_001559:B008/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:12 `ف و ق` (فَوْقَكُمْ); 78:14 `ع ص ر` (ٱلْمُعْصِرَٰتِ), `ن ز ل` (أَنزَلْنَا), `ث ج ج` (ثَجَّاجًۭا); 78:15 `خ ر ج` (لِّنُخْرِجَ); 78:11 `ن ه ر` (ٱلنَّهَارَ).
- Synthesis: The cloud is a pressure-bearing source whose contents arrive in pulses and then break into a concentrated downflow. Emergence, descent, and pouring are successive operations rather than loose weather images.

#### Subchannel B. Catchment, Conduit, and Water Storage
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Released water enters a channel, fills a pit or vessel, is retained, and is then delivered for irrigation.
- Active motifs: water as the working substance (`quranic:root_001458:B001/m01`); water appearing and increasing in wells or soil (`quranic:root_001458:B002/m01`); irrigation and pouring into a receptacle (`quranic:root_001458:B003/m01`); rainwater-holding rock pit or well (`quranic:root_000434:B011/m01`); deep reservoir of abundant water (`quranic:root_001040:B005/m01`); large storage jar (`quranic:root_000286:B007/m01`); bubbles and tracks on water (`quranic:root_000286:B008/m01`); meadow with receiving basins (`quranic:root_000195:B002/m01`); channel cut by running water (`quranic:root_001559:B001/m01`); widening an opening so liquid can flow (`quranic:root_001559:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:4-5 `ع ل م` (سَيَعْلَمُونَ); 78:8 `خ ل ق` (خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ); 78:11 `ن ه ر` (ٱلنَّهَارَ); 78:14 `م و ه` (مَآءً), `ث ج ج` (ثَجَّاجًۭا); 78:15 `ح ب ب` (حَبًّۭا).
- Synthesis: Water does not simply fall; it is held, traced, channeled, and redistributed. Pit, well, jar, basin, and widened outlet assemble a complete hydraulic scene whose output is usable irrigation.

#### Subchannel C. Seed Emergence and Entwined Vegetation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Seed enters fertile ground, emerges, is cultivated, matures within protective envelopes, and forms a dense interlaced garden.
- Active motifs: fertile productive ground (`quranic:root_000025:B002/m01`); cereal or seed grain (`quranic:root_000286:B001/m01`); plant emerging from earth (`quranic:root_001465:B001/m01`); deliberate planting and cultivation (`quranic:root_001465:B002/m01`); place and origin of growth (`quranic:root_001465:B005/m01`); named yanbūt tree (`quranic:root_001465:B007/m01`); emergence from a containing place (`quranic:root_000400:B001/m01`); hidden garden enclosed by trees (`quranic:root_000266:B003/m01`); vegetation thickening and intertwining (`quranic:root_000266:B011/m01`); interlaced trees and grasses (`quranic:root_001365:B002/m01`); renewed crop after loss (`quranic:root_000433:B010/m01`); crop enclosed in its husks (`quranic:root_001019:B010/m01`); named shrub (`quranic:root_001019:B013/m01`); marsh-mallow-like plant (`quranic:root_000665:B009/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:3 `خ ل ف` (مُخْتَلِفُونَ); 78:6 `ء ر ض` (ٱلْأَرْضَ); 78:9 `س ب ت` (سُبَاتًۭا); 78:14 `ع ص ر` (ٱلْمُعْصِرَٰتِ); 78:15 `خ ر ج` (لِّنُخْرِجَ), `ح ب ب` (حَبًّۭا), `ن ب ت` (نَبَاتًۭا); 78:16 `ج ن ن` (جَنَّٰتٍ), `ل ف ف` (أَلْفَافًا).
- Synthesis: Grain, fertile substrate, planted stock, protective husk, and returning crop form a cultivation sequence. Its terminal state is not generic greenery but vegetation dense enough to hide space and interlock with itself.

#### Subchannel D. Extracted Yield, Swallowing, and Bodily Increase
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Plant and liquid yield are extracted, portioned into drink and food, swallowed, and converted into life-supporting bodily substance.
- Active motifs: livelihood and means of subsistence (`quranic:root_001067:B002/m01`); food building flesh (`quranic:root_000156:B010/m01`); pressing fruit or oil for liquid yield (`quranic:root_001019:B002/m01`); extracted produce or gift (`quranic:root_001019:B007/m01`); small drink to pass a choking morsel (`quranic:root_001019:B008/m01`); drinking to fullness (`quranic:root_000286:B006/m01`); watery ripeness and sheen of fruit (`quranic:root_001458:B006/m01`); eating mixed kinds exhaustively (`quranic:root_001365:B009/m01`); a specific strip of camel flesh (`quranic:root_001365:B010/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:11 `ع ي ش` (مَعَاشًۭا); 78:12 `ب ن ي` (بَنَيْنَا); 78:14 `ع ص ر` (ٱلْمُعْصِرَٰتِ), `م و ه` (مَآءً); 78:15 `ح ب ب` (حَبًّۭا); 78:16 `ل ف ف` (أَلْفَافًا).
- Synthesis: Agricultural abundance becomes metabolic: pressing extracts yield, drink clears the throat, food builds flesh, and ripened water-content marks readiness for consumption. The daylight “livelihood” is thereby grounded in a concrete chain from field to body.

### 5. P1 (78:1-16): Pairing Generates, Conceals, and Matures New Life
- Semantic invariant: Counterparts are joined, reproductive material is enclosed, and offspring pass through emergence and maturation into social continuity.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:8 creates “you” as pairs, while 78:15-16 immediately supplies seed, growth, and enclosing gardens as a plant analogue.
- Surprising reach: Pairing expands into marriage, bodily union, semen, gestation, lactation, childhood, and the classification of peers and kinds.

#### Subchannel A. Counterpart, Marriage, and Bodily Union
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Two counterparts are joined as a pair, formalized as spouses, and brought into intimate bodily association.
- Active motifs: each member of a paired two (`quranic:root_000652:B001/m01`); marital spouse (`quranic:root_000652:B002/m01`); joining one counterpart to another (`quranic:root_000652:B003/m01`); entering upon one’s spouse (`quranic:root_000156:B006/m01`); intimate association of spouses (`quranic:root_001341:B004/m01`); tightened bond (`quranic:root_000782:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:8 `ز و ج` (أَزْوَٰجًۭا); 78:10 `ل ب س` (لِبَاسًۭا); 78:12 `ب ن ي` (بَنَيْنَا), `ش د د` (شِدَادًۭا).
- Synthesis: “Pairs” supplies both the two counterpart positions and the act that joins them. Marriage, entry, intimate association, and binding make the relation social, bodily, and durable.

#### Subchannel B. Conception, Gestation, and Emergence
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Desire initiates mating, reproductive fluid enters an enclosing womb, the fetus remains hidden, and new life emerges.
- Active motifs: female animal seeking the male (`quranic:root_000248:B009/m01`); semen placed in the womb (`quranic:root_001458:B004/m01`); hidden fetus in the abdomen (`quranic:root_000266:B007/m01`); beginning or first stage of a thing (`quranic:root_000266:B014/m01`); male fluid discharged (`quranic:root_001492:B009/m01`); emergence from confinement (`quranic:root_000400:B001/m02`); ostrich chick (`quranic:root_000248:B010/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:6, 78:9-11, 78:13 `ج ع ل` (نَجْعَلِ/جَعَلْنَا); 78:14 `م و ه` (مَآءً), `ن ز ل` (أَنزَلْنَا); 78:15 `خ ر ج` (لِّنُخْرِجَ); 78:16 `ج ن ن` (جَنَّٰتٍ).
- Synthesis: The same enclosure-emergence logic that governs rain and vegetation also forms a reproductive scene. Fluid is placed, life is hidden through an initial stage, and the contained being finally crosses outward.

#### Subchannel C. Maturity, Nurture, and Successive Generation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Young life reaches bodily maturity, is nourished and reared, and becomes the next social generation.
- Active motifs: reaching full strength and maturity (`quranic:root_000782:B004/m01`); girl reaching youthful maturity (`quranic:root_001019:B009/m01`); child growth and upbringing (`quranic:root_001465:B006/m01`); young generation added to a people (`quranic:root_001465:B003/m01`); sonship and descent from an origin (`quranic:root_000156:B007/m01`); small offshoots named sons or daughters (`quranic:root_000156:B008/m01`); milk returning between milkings (`quranic:root_001188:B006/m01`); food increasing flesh (`quranic:root_000156:B010/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:12 `ش د د` (شِدَادًۭا), `ب ن ي` (بَنَيْنَا), `ف و ق` (فَوْقَكُمْ); 78:14 `ع ص ر` (ٱلْمُعْصِرَٰتِ); 78:15 `ن ب ت` (نَبَاتًۭا).
- Synthesis: Growth is simultaneously bodily, nutritional, genealogical, and vegetal. Maturity, milk, food-built flesh, and the “new growth” of a community produce a continuous succession rather than a one-time act of creation.

#### Subchannel D. Kinds, Peers, and Differentiated Pairing
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A larger field is sorted into kinds, matched peers, and visually differentiated counterparts.
- Active motifs: kind or class of a thing (`quranic:root_000652:B004/m01`); peers joined by form or conduct (`quranic:root_000652:B005/m01`); two-color differentiation (`quranic:root_000400:B007/m01`); divergent conditions and colors (`quranic:root_000433:B004/m02`); small paired palms (`quranic:root_000248:B006/m01`); collective mixture of different origins (`quranic:root_001365:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:3 `خ ل ف` (مُخْتَلِفُونَ); 78:6, 78:9-11, 78:13 `ج ع ل` (نَجْعَلِ/جَعَلْنَا); 78:8 `ز و ج` (أَزْوَٰجًۭا); 78:15 `خ ر ج` (لِّنُخْرِجَ); 78:16 `ل ف ف` (أَلْفَافًا).
- Synthesis: Pairing does not erase difference; it organizes it. Kind, peer-group, mixed collective, and contrasting color show how plurality can be matched without becoming uniform.

### 6. P1 (78:1-16): Route Preparation, Transit, and Emplacement
- Semantic invariant: Movement requires a traversable surface, readable passage, fitted conveyance, and a transition from arrival to settled placement.
- Surface relation: indirect; the earth-bed and mountain-pegs of 78:6-7 supply terrain, while 78:10-11 supplies night travel and daylight activity.
- Surprising reach: The pericope’s stable world doubles as a road system with mountain passes, route marks, saddled animals, halted travelers, strangers, and successors taking a vacated place.

#### Subchannel A. Clear Road Through Ground and Mountain
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Easy ground carries a marked route through a constricted mountain opening into a broader space.
- Active motifs: clear road over easy land (`quranic:root_001464:B007/m01`); passage from constriction to openness (`quranic:root_000433:B012/m01`); prepared level surface (`quranic:root_001451:B002/m01`); entering mountain terrain (`quranic:root_000217:B006/m01`); broad ridge of sand (`quranic:root_000217:B011/m01`); route marker (`quranic:root_001040:B002/m01`); lower ground (`quranic:root_000025:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:2 `ن ب ء` (ٱلنَّبَإِ); 78:3 `خ ل ف` (مُخْتَلِفُونَ); 78:4-5 `ع ل م` (سَيَعْلَمُونَ); 78:6 `ء ر ض` (ٱلْأَرْضَ), `م ه د` (مِهَٰدًۭا); 78:7 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالَ).
- Synthesis: The prepared earth is traversable infrastructure. A visible mark identifies the route, and the mountain is not only a barrier: it contains a pass that turns a narrow approach into open ground.

#### Subchannel B. Night Gait, Saddle, and Halted Mount
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A mounted traveler moves at night with a fitted saddle and distinct gait until exhaustion or injury forces the animal to stop.
- Active motifs: traveling by night (`quranic:root_001392:B002/m01`); specialized animal gait (`quranic:root_000665:B004/m01`); saddle and saddling (`quranic:root_000693:B002/m01`); charge or rapid running (`quranic:root_000782:B003/m01`); camel halted by incapacity (`quranic:root_000286:B005/m01`); broad-backed saddle condition (`quranic:root_001451:B004/m01`); protruding neck pulling the rein (`quranic:root_000400:B013/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:6 `م ه د` (مِهَٰدًۭا); 78:9 `س ب ت` (سُبَاتًۭا); 78:10 `ل ي ل` (ٱلْيْلَ); 78:12 `ش د د` (شِدَادًۭا); 78:13 `س ر ج` (سِرَاجًۭا); 78:15 `خ ر ج` (لِّنُخْرِجَ), `ح ب ب` (حَبًّۭا).
- Synthesis: Transit is rendered through the mount’s mechanics: saddle, gait, neck, charge, and bodily limit. The night that covers rest also becomes the operating time of a moving caravan.

#### Subchannel C. Stranger, Arrival, Rooting, and Replacement
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An outsider arrives from another place, attaches to land or community, and either takes root or succeeds what has departed.
- Active motifs: stranger called a son of the earth (`quranic:root_000025:B004/m01`); movement from one land to another (`quranic:root_001464:B001/m01`); newcomer or emerging generation (`quranic:root_001465:B003/m01`); plant taking root in earth (`quranic:root_001465:B001/m01`); sonship traced to a source (`quranic:root_000156:B007/m01`); successor taking another’s place (`quranic:root_000433:B001/m01`); laggards remaining behind (`quranic:root_000433:B003/m01`); alternating arrivals (`quranic:root_000433:B006/m01`); clinging to the ground and delaying departure (`quranic:root_000025:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:2 `ن ب ء` (ٱلنَّبَإِ); 78:3 `خ ل ف` (مُخْتَلِفُونَ); 78:6 `ء ر ض` (ٱلْأَرْضَ); 78:12 `ب ن ي` (بَنَيْنَا); 78:15 `ن ب ت` (نَبَاتًۭا).
- Synthesis: Arrival becomes emplacement: the foreign “son of earth” may root like a plant or enter a genealogy. Succession and lagging show the social consequences of movement, since one arrival can occupy the place another leaves.

### 7. P1 (78:1-16): Collective Formation, Lineage, and Rank
- Semantic invariant: Individuals become a collective through aggregation and descent, then acquire differentiated positions of honor, authority, or subordination.
- Surface relation: indirect; 78:3 presents a disputing group and 78:8 addresses humanity as paired plurality.
- Surprising reach: Crowd imagery grows from mountain mass and entwined mixture, while lineage becomes “root,” “growth,” and built descent before hardening into rank and coercive height.

#### Subchannel A. Mountain-Mass Crowd and Mixed Assembly
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Numerous persons gather into a dark, dense, heterogeneous mass.
- Active motifs: multitude as large as a mountain (`quranic:root_000217:B002/m01`); dark mass of people (`quranic:root_000266:B013/m01`); mixed tribes folded into one group (`quranic:root_001365:B003/m01`); peers associated by form or action (`quranic:root_000652:B005/m01`); the greater part or bulk (`quranic:root_001029:B002/m01`); new generation joining a people (`quranic:root_001465:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:7 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالَ); 78:8 `ز و ج` (أَزْوَٰجًۭا); 78:15 `ن ب ت` (نَبَاتًۭا); 78:16 `ج ن ن` (جَنَّٰتٍ), `ل ف ف` (أَلْفَافًا).
- Synthesis: The collective has weight, darkness, and internal mixture. Mountain bulk and entwining convert social number into a dense body while still preserving the different tribes and peer-groups folded within it.

#### Subchannel B. Descent, Root, and Inherited Disposition
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A community traces continuity through parentage, origin, natural disposition, and the place from which growth begins.
- Active motifs: descent and sonship from a source (`quranic:root_000156:B007/m01`); lineage and noble origin (`quranic:root_001019:B011/m01`); growth-origin or native ground (`quranic:root_001465:B005/m01`); innate created disposition (`quranic:root_000217:B004/m01`); inward character or nature (`quranic:root_000434:B004/m01`); successor replacing a predecessor (`quranic:root_000433:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:3 `خ ل ف` (مُخْتَلِفُونَ); 78:7 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالَ); 78:8 `خ ل ق` (خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ); 78:12 `ب ن ي` (بَنَيْنَا); 78:14 `ع ص ر` (ٱلْمُعْصِرَٰتِ); 78:15 `ن ب ت` (نَبَاتًۭا).
- Synthesis: Lineage is both genealogical and ecological: a person descends from a source and grows from a native bed. Innate disposition and succession explain how continuity can persist even as individual occupants change.

#### Subchannel C. Honor, Height, and Coercive Superiority
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Esteem rises into social height, but height can shift from recognized honor to domination and compulsion.
- Active motifs: greatness and power (`quranic:root_001029:B001/m01`); honor and inviolable standing (`quranic:root_001029:B010/m01`); awe before magnitude (`quranic:root_001029:B007/m01`); self-magnifying pride (`quranic:root_001029:B006/m01`); superiority of rank (`quranic:root_001188:B002/m01`); overpowering height (`quranic:root_001188:B003/m01`); chiefs and scholars called mountains (`quranic:root_000217:B012/m01`); forcing another into an action (`quranic:root_000217:B010/m01`); strong coercive capacity (`quranic:root_000782:B002/m02`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:2 `ع ظ م` (ٱلْعَظِيمِ); 78:7 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالَ); 78:12 `ف و ق` (فَوْقَكُمْ), `ش د د` (شِدَادًۭا).
- Synthesis: Vertical magnitude becomes a social grammar. The honored “mountain” can stabilize a people, yet superiority also permits overpowering and forced compliance, making greatness ethically double-edged.

### 8. P1 (78:1-16): Applied Force, Extraction, and Material Failure
- Semantic invariant: Force can secure an assembly, express a useful yield, overcome resistance, or damage the material it acts upon.
- Surface relation: indirect; pegs and strong structures in 78:7 and 78:12 foreground controlled force, while the forceful rain of 78:14 supplies pressure and release.
- Surprising reach: The same mechanics span bowstrings, knots, presses, channels, combat charges, blocked excavation, worn cloth, termites, and twisted working limbs.

#### Subchannel A. Tensioned Fastening and Directed Release
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Components are fixed under tension so that stored force can stabilize a structure or propel a directed object.
- Active motifs: tightened knot and bond (`quranic:root_000782:B001/m01`); driven peg (`quranic:root_001620:B001/m01`); bow adhering tightly to its string (`quranic:root_000156:B005/m01`); arrow nock receiving the string (`quranic:root_001188:B009/m01`); tightly woven fabric (`quranic:root_000217:B007/m01`); wrapping under tension (`quranic:root_001365:B001/m01`); fixed upright support (`quranic:root_001620:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:7 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالَ), `و ت د` (أَوْتَادًۭا); 78:12 `ب ن ي` (بَنَيْنَا), `ش د د` (شِدَادًۭا), `ف و ق` (فَوْقَكُمْ); 78:16 `ل ف ف` (أَلْفَافًا).
- Synthesis: Peg, knot, weave, bow, and arrow nock share a precise operation: force is stored by joining components under tension. Depending on the assembly, that force holds a frame still or releases a projectile.

#### Subchannel B. Compression That Expresses Yield
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Pressure is applied to fruit, property, or a constricted outlet until liquid or usable value is forced out.
- Active motifs: pressing grapes or oil (`quranic:root_001019:B002/m01`); extracted yield or income (`quranic:root_001019:B007/m01`); withholding and extracting another’s property (`quranic:root_001019:B006/m01`); forceful outpour (`quranic:root_000195:B001/m01`); widening an outlet for flow (`quranic:root_001559:B003/m01`); bringing a concealed object out (`quranic:root_000400:B002/m02`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:11 `ن ه ر` (ٱلنَّهَارَ); 78:14 `ع ص ر` (ٱلْمُعْصِرَٰتِ), `ث ج ج` (ثَجَّاجًۭا); 78:15 `خ ر ج` (لِّنُخْرِجَ).
- Synthesis: Compression has a productive endpoint when it expresses liquid or recoverable value. Yet the same operation can become coercive when property is withheld and squeezed from another, distinguishing extraction from generous production.

#### Subchannel C. Resistance, Charge, and Forced Submission
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hardness blocks entry, an attacker applies concentrated force, and resistance is overcome by charge or compulsion.
- Active motifs: hard ground stopping excavation (`quranic:root_000217:B005/m01`); obstruction and prevention (`quranic:root_000217:B009/m02`); compelling another to an act (`quranic:root_000217:B010/m01`); forceful attack and running (`quranic:root_000782:B003/m01`); predatory animal (`quranic:root_000669:B002/m01`); extreme taking or punishment (`quranic:root_000669:B005/m01`); descent for single combat (`quranic:root_001492:B007/m01`); overpowering superiority (`quranic:root_001188:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:7 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالَ); 78:12 `ش د د` (شِدَادًۭا), `ف و ق` (فَوْقَكُمْ), `س ب ع` (سَبْعًۭا); 78:14 `ن ز ل` (أَنزَلْنَا).
- Synthesis: Solidity first appears as resistance to penetration. Charge, predation, combat descent, and coercive height then supply the concentrated actions by which one body attempts to break another’s resistance.

#### Subchannel D. Wear, Infestation, and Disabled Material
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Fabric, wood, flesh, or a working limb loses integrity through wear, consumption, corruption, or internal twisting.
- Active motifs: termite consuming wood (`quranic:root_000025:B010/m01`); worn cloth losing its nap (`quranic:root_000434:B009/m01`); state changing into corruption (`quranic:root_000433:B007/m01`); festering sore (`quranic:root_000025:B011/m01`); protruding boil or wound (`quranic:root_000400:B004/m01`); twisted forearm vein disabling work (`quranic:root_001365:B007/m01`); decayed market or garment (`quranic:root_001568:B007/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:3 `خ ل ف` (مُخْتَلِفُونَ); 78:6 `ء ر ض` (ٱلْأَرْضَ); 78:8 `خ ل ق` (خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ); 78:9 `ن و م` (نَوْمَكُمْ); 78:15 `خ ر ج` (لِّنُخْرِجَ); 78:16 `ل ف ف` (أَلْفَافًا).
- Synthesis: Structural strength is pressured by a coherent failure sequence: surface wear opens weakness, an agent consumes material, corruption spreads, and a working part becomes unusable. The scene is the inverse of fastening and load-bearing.

### 9. P1 (78:1-16): Heat, Radiance, and the Trustworthiness of Surfaces
- Semantic invariant: Energy becomes visible or sensible as heat, light, lustre, scent, and applied finish, but the resulting surface may either reveal quality or disguise the substrate.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:13 names a blazing lamp and 78:10 places it against night’s concealing cover.
- Surprising reach: Illumination reaches jewel-glint, perfume plume, polished glass, cosmetic coating, metal plating, mottled surfaces, and useless sparks.

#### Subchannel A. Combustion, Lamp, and Useful Heat
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A lamp converts fuel into light and heat, while protective handling distinguishes sustained use from weak, useless sparks.
- Active motifs: oil-fed illuminating lamp (`quranic:root_000693:B001/m01`); blazing solar or fire heat (`quranic:root_001686:B001/m01`); weak sparks with no useful heat (`quranic:root_000286:B011/m01`); cloth used to lower a hot pot (`quranic:root_000248:B007/m01`); making or producing an object (`quranic:root_000248:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:6, 78:9-11, 78:13 `ج ع ل` (نَجْعَلِ/جَعَلْنَا); 78:13 `س ر ج` (سِرَاجًۭا), `و ه ج` (وَهَّاجًۭا); 78:15 `ح ب ب` (حَبًّۭا).
- Synthesis: The blazing lamp is an energy-conversion apparatus whose output must be sustained and handled. The useless spark is its failure mode: visible ignition without durable benefit.

#### Subchannel B. Lustre, Fragrance, and Sensory Emanation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A source announces its condition by sending out reflected light, sheen, or fragrance.
- Active motifs: jewel flashing with light (`quranic:root_001686:B002/m01`); perfume diffusing its scent (`quranic:root_001686:B003/m01`); beautified radiant face (`quranic:root_000693:B003/m01`); watery sheen in face or speech (`quranic:root_001458:B006/m02`); crystal or mirror-like clarity (`quranic:root_001458:B007/m01`); perfuming or painting with khalūq (`quranic:root_000434:B010/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:8 `خ ل ق` (خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ); 78:13 `س ر ج` (سِرَاجًۭا), `و ه ج` (وَهَّاجًۭا); 78:14 `م و ه` (مَآءً).
- Synthesis: Light and scent behave alike as emanations that make an interior quality perceptible at a distance. Face, jewel, fruit, mirror, and perfume all convert material condition into a sensory signal.

#### Subchannel C. Decorative Finish, Mottling, and False Appearance
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A surface is colored, patterned, polished, or plated; the finish can classify and beautify, but it can also misrepresent what lies beneath.
- Active motifs: plating base metal with gold or silver and disguising reality (`quranic:root_001458:B005/m01`); two-color mottling (`quranic:root_000400:B007/m01`); kind distinguished as a color or class (`quranic:root_000652:B004/m01`); perfumed cosmetic coating (`quranic:root_000434:B010/m01`); beautifying and regularizing a face (`quranic:root_000693:B003/m01`); liar figured as a lamp (`quranic:root_000693:B005/m01`); polished, solid surface (`quranic:root_000434:B008/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:8 `خ ل ق` (خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ), `ز و ج` (أَزْوَٰجًۭا); 78:13 `س ر ج` (سِرَاجًۭا); 78:14 `م و ه` (مَآءً); 78:15 `خ ر ج` (لِّنُخْرِجَ).
- Synthesis: Surface treatment can reveal type and craft, but plating and “lamp-like” lying make appearance epistemically unstable. The channel therefore separates legitimate finish from a bright surface that conceals a different substrate.

### 10. P1 (78:1-16): The Body as Frame, Sensor, and Vulnerable Interior
- Semantic invariant: Bodily components mirror architectural supports and apertures, while injury or impaired interior states expose the limits of that frame.
- Surface relation: indirect; bodily creation in 78:8 stands amid beds, pegs, and built structures in 78:6-12.
- Surprising reach: The created body becomes a ribbed house with projecting pegs, a heart-like hidden chamber, and failure modes ranging from wounds to dulled awareness.

#### Subchannel A. Thoracic Frame, Bone, and Projection
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Ribs, thick bone, projecting joints, and upright protuberances form a load-bearing bodily architecture.
- Active motifs: chest ribs and bones (`quranic:root_000266:B016/m01`); hard bone (`quranic:root_001029:B005/m01`); thick part of a limb (`quranic:root_001029:B003/m02`); ribs as supports (`quranic:root_000156:B009/m01`); ear projection like a peg (`quranic:root_001620:B002/m01`); upper joint or cleft in body and tool (`quranic:root_001188:B013/m01`); thick dry bodily formation (`quranic:root_000217:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:2 `ع ظ م` (ٱلْعَظِيمِ); 78:7 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالَ), `و ت د` (أَوْتَادًۭا); 78:8 `خ ل ق` (خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ); 78:12 `ب ن ي` (بَنَيْنَا), `ف و ق` (فَوْقَكُمْ); 78:16 `ج ن ن` (جَنَّٰتٍ).
- Synthesis: The body repeats the pericope’s built world at another scale. Bone and ribs bear load, while ear and joint project or cleave like pegs and fitted construction points.

#### Subchannel B. Breach, Swelling, and Obstructed Function
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A bodily boundary is breached or sealed incorrectly, producing wound, swelling, corruption, or disabled movement.
- Active motifs: boil or sore protruding from the body (`quranic:root_000400:B004/m01`); festering lesion (`quranic:root_000025:B011/m01`); sealed bodily opening like smooth rock (`quranic:root_000434:B012/m01`); twisted vein disabling the forearm (`quranic:root_001365:B007/m01`); visible cleft in the upper lip (`quranic:root_001040:B004/m01`); tremor or bodily shudder (`quranic:root_000025:B008/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:4-5 `ع ل م` (سَيَعْلَمُونَ); 78:6 `ء ر ض` (ٱلْأَرْضَ); 78:8 `خ ل ق` (خَلَقْنَٰكُمْ); 78:15 `خ ر ج` (لِّنُخْرِجَ); 78:16 `ل ف ف` (أَلْفَافًا).
- Synthesis: The healthy frame depends on correctly open and correctly closed boundaries. A sore pushes outward, a cleft remains visible, a sealed aperture blocks passage, and a twisted internal line disables the limb.

#### Subchannel C. Hidden Heart, Dulled Awareness, and Mental Cover
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The interior seat of awareness is hidden, then covered or disturbed until perception and judgment become unreliable.
- Active motifs: hidden heart or inner fear (`quranic:root_000266:B010/m01`); black core of the heart (`quranic:root_000286:B004/m01`); mind covered by madness (`quranic:root_000266:B006/m01`); dull, water-heavy heart (`quranic:root_001458:B008/m01`); obscurity and heedlessness (`quranic:root_001568:B004/m01`); involuntary derangement attributed to earth-beings (`quranic:root_000025:B012/m01`); feigned sleep (`quranic:root_001568:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:6 `ء ر ض` (ٱلْأَرْضَ); 78:9 `ن و م` (نَوْمَكُمْ); 78:14 `م و ه` (مَآءً); 78:15 `ح ب ب` (حَبًّۭا); 78:16 `ج ن ن` (جَنَّٰتٍ).
- Synthesis: Concealment shifts from a protective exterior to a disturbed interior. The heart is a hidden chamber, but madness, dullness, heedlessness, and pretended sleep show concealment becoming a failure of awareness rather than shelter.

### 11. P2 (78:17-30): Fixed Appointment Becoming Decisive Judgment
- Semantic invariant: A severe event is assigned a bounded time, arrives when due, and terminates uncertainty through a separating verdict.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:17 names the Day of Separation as a fixed appointment, and 78:26-30 presents its matching recompense, account, record, and sentence.
- Surprising reach: Timekeeping becomes courtroom procedure: a deadline matures into a verdict, while deferred expectation is overtaken by an event whose “fit” includes punctuality.

#### Subchannel A. Bounded Day, Appointed Limit, and Severe Event
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A period is delimited, fixed as an appointment, and realized as a severe occurrence in time.
- Active motifs: bounded daylight interval (`quranic:root_001700:B001/m01`); any extended duration (`quranic:root_001700:B002/m01`); severe day or epochal event (`quranic:root_001700:B003/m01`); known time and endpoint (`quranic:root_001671:B001/m01`); assigning a temporal limit (`quranic:root_001671:B002/m01`); formally appointed time (`quranic:root_001671:B003/m01`); occurrence and presence in time (`quranic:root_001332:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:17 `ي و م` (يَوْمَ), `و ق ت` (مِيقَٰتًۭا), `ك و ن` (كَانَ); 78:18 `ي و م` (يَوْمَ); 78:19-21, 78:27 `ك و ن` (كَانَتْ/كَانُوا۟).
- Synthesis: Day, duration, occurrence, and appointment produce a complete timing apparatus. The event is not merely future; its endpoint is set, its interval is bounded, and its severity becomes present when that limit is reached.

#### Subchannel B. Separation, Verdict, and Enacted Decree
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Contested material is distinguished into parts, judged, explained, and fixed as an enforceable determination.
- Active motifs: cutting a clear boundary between things (`quranic:root_001159:B001/m01`); verdict separating right from false (`quranic:root_001159:B002/m01`); detailed clarification of parts and meanings (`quranic:root_001159:B013/m01`); judicial opening between litigants (`quranic:root_001124:B003/m01`); written decree imposing judgment (`quranic:root_001283:B003/m01`); fitting time at which an event falls due (`quranic:root_001668:B005/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:17 `ف ص ل` (ٱلْفَصْلِ), `و ق ت` (مِيقَٰتًۭا); 78:19 `ف ت ح` (فُتِحَتِ); 78:26 `و ف ق` (وِفَاقًا); 78:29 `ك ت ب` (كِتَٰبًۭا).
- Synthesis: Separation is simultaneously analytic and judicial: distinctions are made visible, their meaning is stated, and the result is fixed as decree. Punctual “fit” links the correctness of the decision to the moment of its execution.

#### Subchannel C. Hope, Deferment, and the Arrival That Ends Delay
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A subject hopes, postpones, or estimates, but the approaching event arrives and replaces uncertain expectation with experienced fact.
- Active motifs: hopeful orientation toward an expected good (`quranic:root_000548:B001/m01`); deferring a matter to a later time (`quranic:root_000548:B004/m01`); estimate or supposition (`quranic:root_000318:B002/m01`); impending matter whose time has come (`quranic:root_000001:B006/m01`); coming calamity or destruction (`quranic:root_000009:B011/m01`); arriving and reaching (`quranic:root_000009:B001/m01`); direct experience replacing abstraction (`quranic:root_000526:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:18 `ء ت ي` (فَتَأْتُونَ); 78:25 `ح م م` (حَمِيمًۭا); 78:27 `ر ج و` (يَرْجُونَ), `ح س ب` (حِسَابًۭا); 78:30 `ذ و ق` (فَذُوقُوا۟).
- Synthesis: The denial of expected accounting is pressured by a broader sequence of expectation, postponement, imminence, arrival, and experience. Once the event comes, conjecture is no longer the governing cognitive state.

### 12. P2 (78:17-30): A Pneumatic Summons Mobilizing Routes and Cohorts
- Semantic invariant: A burst of directed air activates a signal, which initiates movement along routes and gathers dispersed participants into successive formations.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:18 joins the blowing of the horn to the arrival of people in cohorts.
- Surprising reach: The summons behaves like a transport network involving airflow, swelling, road junctions, couriers, returning limbs, caravans, herds, and broad passages.

#### Subchannel A. Blown Horn, Inflation, and Expanding Force
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Breath or wind enters a resonant vessel, expands it or what it addresses, and releases an activating signal.
- Active motifs: horn used for blowing (`quranic:root_000891:B004/m01`); sending breath or wind into a thing (`quranic:root_001529:B001/m01`); swelling through retained air (`quranic:root_001529:B002/m01`); raised or swelling ground and day (`quranic:root_001529:B003/m01`); pride inflated by wind (`quranic:root_001529:B006/m01`); formed image or configuration (`quranic:root_000891:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:18 `ن ف خ` (يُنفَخُ), `ص و ر` (ٱلصُّورِ).
- Synthesis: The horn scene is a pressure mechanism: air enters, volume expands, and force becomes signal. The pride branch preserves a social analogue in which inner inflation produces an enlarged outward stance.

#### Subchannel B. Arrival Through Roads, Channels, and Returning Motion
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Travelers approach by a public route or water-like channel, their limbs repeat a returning gait, and a courier or caravan carries movement onward.
- Active motifs: coming and reaching (`quranic:root_000009:B001/m01`); channel directing water to a basin (`quranic:root_000009:B004/m01`); flood arriving from another region (`quranic:root_000009:B005/m01`); outsider entering another people (`quranic:root_000009:B006/m01`); returning forelimbs in gait (`quranic:root_000009:B009/m01`); public road and junction (`quranic:root_000009:B010/m01`); repeated limb movement in return (`quranic:root_000065:B003/m01`); arrival by night (`quranic:root_000065:B006/m01`); courier’s rapid coming and going (`quranic:root_000103:B004/m01`); forward travel and flow (`quranic:root_000769:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:18 `ء ت ي` (فَتَأْتُونَ); 78:20 `س ي ر` (سُيِّرَتِ); 78:22 `ء و ب` (مَـَٔابًۭا); 78:24 `ب ر د` (بَرْدًۭا).
- Synthesis: Arrival is distributed across roadway, flood channel, courier line, and the body of a moving animal. The repeated return of limbs makes collective coming a coordinated gait rather than an instantaneous appearance.

#### Subchannel C. Cohorts, Caravans, Herds, and Broad Passages
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Individuals are collected into successive human groups whose form is mirrored by moving caravans, herds, and animal files.
- Active motifs: human cohort arriving group after group (`quranic:root_001184:B001/m01`); broad space between raised formations (`quranic:root_001184:B002/m01`); mountain-sized multitude (`quranic:root_000217:B002/m01`); herd or moving animal unit (`quranic:root_000691:B005/m01`); cattle herd (`quranic:root_000891:B006/m01`); traveling caravan (`quranic:root_000769:B006/m01`); community gathered to drink (`quranic:root_000783:B003/m01`); crowds called kalākil (`quranic:root_001315:B009/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:18 `ف و ج` (أَفْوَاجًۭا), `ص و ر` (ٱلصُّورِ), `ء ت ي` (فَتَأْتُونَ); 78:20 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالُ), `س ي ر` (سُيِّرَتِ), `س ر ب` (سَرَابًا); 78:24 `ش ر ب` (شَرَابًا); 78:29 `ك ل ل` (كُلَّ).
- Synthesis: “Cohorts” is a formation type with spacing, sequence, and direction. Caravans and herds supply concrete analogues for how a multitude can move as discrete units without losing collective order.

### 13. P2 (78:17-30): Closed Boundaries Open, Move, and Become Permeable
- Semantic invariant: Formerly stable enclosures and hard masses lose closure through opening, displacement, channeling, or optical dissolution.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:19 turns the opened sky into doors and 78:20 moves mountains until they become mirage.
- Surprising reach: Apertures, keys, mountain clefts, leaking channels, eroded hardness, and false water all participate in a single unmaking of fixed boundaries.

#### Subchannel A. Sky Enclosure Converted into Access Points
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An elevated covering opens, its aperture widens, and the continuous enclosure is divided into multiple entries.
- Active motifs: sky or overhead covering (`quranic:root_000745:B004/m01`); elevation and visibility (`quranic:root_000745:B001/m01`); doorway or entry (`quranic:root_000163:B001/m01`); partitioning into doors or classes (`quranic:root_000163:B002/m01`); boundary gate or terminal opening (`quranic:root_000163:B004/m01`); closed aperture widening (`quranic:root_001124:B001/m01`); key or access instrument (`quranic:root_001124:B006/m01`); occurrence of a changed state (`quranic:root_001332:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:19 `ف ت ح` (فُتِحَتِ), `س م و` (ٱلسَّمَآءُ), `ك و ن` (فَكَانَتْ), `ب و ب` (أَبْوَٰبًۭا).
- Synthesis: Sky changes from one continuous covering into a plurality of controlled access points. Door, boundary, aperture, and key describe distinct parts of the opening operation rather than merely repeating “openness.”

#### Subchannel B. Mountain Solidity Displaced into Mirage
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A thick immovable mass is set in motion, loses resistance, and terminates as a visually convincing but materially absent water-form.
- Active motifs: raised solid mountain mass (`quranic:root_000217:B001/m01`); thick dry body (`quranic:root_000217:B003/m01`); hardness stopping excavation (`quranic:root_000217:B005/m01`); broad sand ridge (`quranic:root_000217:B011/m01`); compelled removal or exile (`quranic:root_000769:B005/m01`); forward movement (`quranic:root_000769:B001/m01`); mirage shining like water (`quranic:root_000691:B009/m01`); becoming a new state (`quranic:root_001332:B001/m01`); filing and abrading hard material (`quranic:root_000103:B005/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:20 `س ي ر` (سُيِّرَتِ), `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالُ), `ك و ن` (فَكَانَتْ), `س ر ب` (سَرَابًا); 78:24 `ب ر د` (بَرْدًۭا).
- Synthesis: The transformation is measured against the mountain’s lexical maximum of solidity. Motion, abrasion, and optical substitution remove that solidity step by step until only a water-like appearance remains.

#### Subchannel C. Cleft, Channel, and Subterranean Passage
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Water or a moving body finds a cleft in hard terrain, enters a downward passage, and travels through an opened outlet.
- Active motifs: mountain or sand cleft carrying water (`quranic:root_001159:B006/m01`); water issuing from an opened source (`quranic:root_001124:B005/m01`); downward tunnel or burrow (`quranic:root_000691:B002/m01`); seepage and dripping flow (`quranic:root_000691:B003/m01`); channel guiding water (`quranic:root_000009:B004/m01`); incoming flood (`quranic:root_000009:B005/m01`); broad pass between elevations (`quranic:root_001184:B002/m01`); entering mountain terrain (`quranic:root_000217:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:18 `ء ت ي` (فَتَأْتُونَ), `ف و ج` (أَفْوَاجًۭا); 78:19 `ف ت ح` (فُتِحَتِ); 78:20 `ج ب ل` (ٱلْجِبَالُ), `س ر ب` (سَرَابًا); 78:17 `ف ص ل` (ٱلْفَصْلِ).
- Synthesis: Permeability is completed by a precise path geometry: cleft, downward tunnel, channel, outlet, and broad pass. The same topology accommodates water and a moving host.

### 14. P2 (78:17-30): Surveillance, Interception, and Prolonged Retention
- Semantic invariant: A prepared station observes approaching subjects, intercepts them at a controlled threshold, and converts return into a long, restrained stay.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:21 calls Hell a watchpost, 78:22 a return-place, and 78:23 describes prolonged residence.
- Surprising reach: The destination resembles an ambush, gated institution, trap, bathhouse, drinking station, luggage restraint, and tethered route.

#### Subchannel A. Watcher, Ambush, and Prepared Trap
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A watcher occupies a selected route, waits for an approaching target, and uses a prepared place to intercept it.
- Active motifs: watching, guarding, and lying in wait (`quranic:root_000566:B001/m01`); preparing something for a target (`quranic:root_000566:B002/m01`); watchpost, ambush site, or trap (`quranic:root_000566:B003/m01`); gatekeeper controlling entry (`quranic:root_000163:B003/m01`); treasury or repository behind an opening (`quranic:root_001124:B007/m01`); coming and reaching the station (`quranic:root_000009:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:18 `ء ت ي` (فَتَأْتُونَ); 78:19 `ب و ب` (أَبْوَٰبًۭا), `ف ت ح` (فُتِحَتِ); 78:21 `ر ص د` (مِرْصَادًۭا).
- Synthesis: The watchpost is an active interception system, not only a location. Preparation, concealment, route selection, gatekeeping, and arrival define the participant roles of watcher, threshold, and target.

#### Subchannel B. Return-Place as Inverted Hospitality
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A returning guest reaches an entry equipped with bathing and drinking facilities, but those facilities are transformed from reception into exposure.
- Active motifs: return to an abode (`quranic:root_000065:B001/m01`); entrance to a place (`quranic:root_000163:B001/m01`); drinking place, vessel, or chamber (`quranic:root_000783:B004/m01`); circulating water-server (`quranic:root_000163:B006/m01`); bathhouse and heating vessel (`quranic:root_000001:B013/m01`); place and station (`quranic:root_001332:B002/m01`); return from every direction (`quranic:root_000065:B004/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:19 `ب و ب` (أَبْوَٰبًۭا); 78:22 `ء و ب` (مَـَٔابًۭا); 78:24 `ش ر ب` (شَرَابًا); 78:25 `ح م م` (حَمِيمًۭا); 78:17, 78:19-21, 78:27 `ك و ن` (كَانَ/كَانَتْ/كَانُوا۟).
- Synthesis: The return-place carries the equipment of reception but reverses its function. Entry, water service, bath, and drinking chamber no longer restore the traveler; they organize punishment.

#### Subchannel C. Long Stay, Stoppage, and Temporal Retention
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Movement slows into residence, residence becomes confinement, and confinement stretches across successive epochs.
- Active motifs: remaining in a place (`quranic:root_001339:B001/m01`); delay and slowness (`quranic:root_001339:B002/m01`); extended epoch (`quranic:root_000345:B004/m01`); retention or blockage (`quranic:root_000345:B001/m01`); stillness after heat or motion (`quranic:root_000103:B002/m01`); bad condition or state (`quranic:root_001332:B006/m01`); duration of any length (`quranic:root_001700:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:17-18 `ي و م` (يَوْمَ); 78:20-21, 78:27 `ك و ن` (كَانَتْ/كَانُوا۟); 78:23 `ل ب ث` (لَّٰبِثِينَ), `ح ق ب` (أَحْقَابًۭا); 78:24 `ب ر د` (بَرْدًۭا).
- Synthesis: The duration is generated through a kinetic sequence: slowing, stopping, remaining, and then extending. “Epochs” is therefore the temporal measure of a condition already defined as immobilized residence.

#### Subchannel D. Belt, Strap, Tether, and Carried Burden
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A retained subject is secured by waist-belt, leather strap, neck-rope, and a trailing attachment that also bears carried weight.
- Active motifs: belly-belt and securing rope (`quranic:root_000345:B002/m01`); bag carried behind a rider (`quranic:root_000345:B003/m01`); leather strap (`quranic:root_000769:B003/m01`); rope put around an animal’s neck (`quranic:root_000783:B008/m01`); dangling thong or lash-tip (`quranic:root_000994:B006/m01`); provisions carried for travel (`quranic:root_000657:B005/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:20 `س ي ر` (سُيِّرَتِ); 78:23 `ح ق ب` (أَحْقَابًۭا); 78:24 `ش ر ب` (شَرَابًا); 78:30 `ع ذ ب` (عَذَابًا), `ز ي د` (نَّزِيدَكُمْ).
- Synthesis: Retention acquires a transport technology: what once secured saddle, baggage, and provisions becomes a system of bodily restraint. The travel kit is preserved, but its function has shifted from enabling motion to preventing escape.

### 15. P2 (78:17-30): Refreshment Corrupted into Thermal and Gustatory Ordeal
- Semantic invariant: The ordinary sequence of cooling, drinking, tasting, and bodily restoration is inverted into heat, darkness, exudate, and forced experience.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:24-25 denies coolness and drink except scalding and dark fluid, and 78:30 commands the sufferers to taste.
- Surprising reach: The punishment enters the physiology of sweat, fever, melted fat, throat channels, bitter water, leaking vessels, eye-darkening, and wound discharge.

#### Subchannel A. Coolness, Potable Share, and Restoration Withheld
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A heated or moving body seeks cool potable liquid and its allotted share, but both refreshment and stable rest are denied.
- Active motifs: cold and cooling liquid (`quranic:root_000103:B001/m01`); cooling as sleep, rest, or death-like stillness (`quranic:root_000103:B002/m01`); drinking a liquid (`quranic:root_000783:B001/m01`); allotted water-share (`quranic:root_000783:B002/m01`); bitter or brackish drink (`quranic:root_000783:B005/m01`); sweet palatable water (`quranic:root_000994:B001/m01`); body refusing food and drink from thirst (`quranic:root_000994:B002/m01`); tasting food (`quranic:root_000526:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:24 `ذ و ق` (يَذُوقُونَ), `ب ر د` (بَرْدًۭا), `ش ر ب` (شَرَابًا); 78:30 `ذ و ق` (فَذُوقُوا۟), `ع ذ ب` (عَذَابًا).
- Synthesis: Coolness is not a loose contrast to heat; it belongs to a restoration scene with drink, allotted access, palatability, and bodily repose. Each expected restorative function is explicitly reversed or withheld.

#### Subchannel B. Scalding Water, Sweat, Fever, and Melted Fat
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Heat penetrates water and body, producing scalding liquid, sweat, fever, and the liquefaction of fat.
- Active motifs: scalding water and hot spring (`quranic:root_000001:B002/m01`); melted fat and its residue (`quranic:root_000001:B003/m01`); sweat and heat effect (`quranic:root_000001:B004/m01`); bodily fever (`quranic:root_000001:B005/m01`); soot, charcoal, and black smoke (`quranic:root_000001:B001/m01`); intense painful punishment (`quranic:root_000994:B005/m01`); flowing and pouring discharge (`quranic:root_001086:B004/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:25 `ح م م` (حَمِيمًۭا), `غ س ق` (غَسَّاقًۭا); 78:30 `ع ذ ب` (عَذَابًا).
- Synthesis: The hot drink expands into a complete thermal breakdown of material and body. Water scalds, fat melts, skin sweats, fever rises, and combustion leaves smoke and soot.

#### Subchannel C. Darkness, Eye Failure, and Wound Exudate
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Light fails, the eye darkens, and bodily or nocturnal fluid pours out as contaminated discharge.
- Active motifs: darkness entering with night (`quranic:root_001086:B001/m01`); eye losing its light (`quranic:root_001086:B003/m01`); eye or wound discharging fluid (`quranic:root_001086:B004/m02`); impurities mixed into food (`quranic:root_001086:B005/m01`); soot-black smoke (`quranic:root_000001:B001/m01`); mirage-like false visibility (`quranic:root_000691:B009/m01`); bodily channels and traces (`quranic:root_000691:B008/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:20 `س ر ب` (سَرَابًا); 78:25 `غ س ق` (غَسَّاقًۭا), `ح م م` (حَمِيمًۭا).
- Synthesis: Ghasaq is reframed as both failed illumination and corrupt flow. The mirage offers false sight, the eye itself darkens, and the fluid that remains is exudate rather than refreshment.

#### Subchannel D. Taste as Trial Through Mouth, Throat, and Changed State
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The mouth tests a substance, the throat admits or resists it, and the taster is forced into experiential knowledge and a changed condition.
- Active motifs: oral taste (`quranic:root_000526:B001/m01`); experience through affliction (`quranic:root_000526:B002/m01`); testing a bow’s tension (`quranic:root_000526:B003/m01`); becoming a new state after separation (`quranic:root_000526:B006/m01`); moustache and throat channels (`quranic:root_000783:B006/m01`); stretching the neck to see or hear (`quranic:root_000783:B009/m01`); drinking as understanding (`quranic:root_000783:B011/m01`); preparing a waterskin so its seams seal (`quranic:root_000783:B013/m01`); abstention under severe thirst (`quranic:root_000994:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:24 `ذ و ق` (يَذُوقُونَ), `ش ر ب` (شَرَابًا); 78:30 `ذ و ق` (فَذُوقُوا۟), `ع ذ ب` (عَذَابًا).
- Synthesis: “Taste” is an enforced test whose instrument is the whole intake pathway. Mouth, throat, neck, and vessel seams materialize the transition from external substance to inwardly known condition.

### 16. P2 (78:17-30): Exhaustive Ledger and Proportionate Settlement
- Semantic invariant: Discrete items are gathered into a total inventory, inscribed as binding record, and settled by a recompense proportioned to what is recorded.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:26 calls the recompense fitting, 78:27 names account, and 78:29 states that every thing is enumerated in a book.
- Surprising reach: Counting draws on pebbles and mental steadiness, writing on stitching and registry, and settlement on debt collection, tax, substitution, and matched sufficiency.

#### Subchannel A. Countable Thing, Pebble, and Complete Inventory
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Knowable items are individuated, counted one by one, and enclosed in a complete total.
- Active motifs: knowable reportable thing (`quranic:root_000831:B001/m01`); volition (`quranic:root_000832:B001/m01`); all parts gathered into a whole (`quranic:root_001315:B003/m01`); numerical enumeration and exhaustive tally (`quranic:root_000332:B002/m01`); pebble as counting unit (`quranic:root_000332:B001/m01`); arithmetic and reckoning (`quranic:root_000318:B001/m01`); encircling totality like a crown (`quranic:root_001315:B005/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:27 `ح س ب` (حِسَابًۭا); 78:29 `ك ل ل` (كُلَّ), `ش ي ء` (شَىْءٍ), `ح ص ي` (أَحْصَيْنَٰهُ).
- Synthesis: The universal “every thing” is operationalized as an inventory: entities are individuated, represented by countable units, and enclosed in a completed whole. Pebble and crown give tally and totality concrete forms.

#### Subchannel B. Stitching, Script, Registry, and Binding Record
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Elements are joined, letters are ordered, a name enters a register, and the resulting document fixes obligation.
- Active motifs: joining and stitching one thing to another (`quranic:root_001283:B001/m01`); arranging letters into writing (`quranic:root_001283:B002/m01`); inscription as binding decree (`quranic:root_001283:B003/m01`); entering a name in a register (`quranic:root_001283:B004/m01`); contracted manumission record (`quranic:root_001283:B005/m01`); textual separators and verse endings (`quranic:root_001159:B011/m01`); clear sign composed as a unit (`quranic:root_000074:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:17 `ف ص ل` (ٱلْفَصْلِ); 78:28 `ء ي ي` (بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا); 78:29 `ك ت ب` (كِتَٰبًۭا).
- Synthesis: The book is both constructed object and legal instrument. Stitching supplies material joining, script orders signs, registry assigns identity, and the completed record imposes a durable condition.

#### Subchannel C. Equivalent Measure, Sufficiency, and Recompense
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An act is answered by an equal or sufficient return, with no excess beyond the measure that corresponds to it.
- Active motifs: action answered by reward or punishment (`quranic:root_000244:B001/m01`); substitute standing in another’s place (`quranic:root_000244:B002/m01`); debt demanded and recovered (`quranic:root_000244:B003/m01`); victory in reciprocal recompense (`quranic:root_000244:B005/m01`); agreement and mutual fit (`quranic:root_001668:B001/m01`); encounter with the matching thing (`quranic:root_001668:B002/m01`); amount exactly sufficient to need (`quranic:root_001668:B004/m01`); sufficiency and adequacy (`quranic:root_000318:B003/m01`); administrative accounting (`quranic:root_000318:B006/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:26 `ج ز ي` (جَزَآءًۭ), `و ف ق` (وِفَاقًا); 78:27 `ح س ب` (حِسَابًۭا).
- Synthesis: “Fitting” recompense is a relation of measured correspondence. Action, substitute, debt, need, and return are aligned so the settlement answers what precedes it rather than merely following it.

#### Subchannel D. Tax, Levy, and Contracted Obligation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A quantified liability is imposed, entered into a contract, and collected at its due time.
- Active motifs: tribute delivered to authority (`quranic:root_000009:B008/m01`); poll-tax or assessed levy (`quranic:root_000244:B004/m01`); collecting an outstanding debt (`quranic:root_000244:B003/m01`); written legal obligation (`quranic:root_001283:B003/m01`); staged payment contract (`quranic:root_001283:B005/m01`); bidding for an increased amount (`quranic:root_000657:B004/m01`); fixed payment deadline (`quranic:root_001671:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:18 `ء ت ي` (فَتَأْتُونَ); 78:26 `ج ز ي` (جَزَآءًۭ); 78:29 `ك ت ب` (كِتَٰبًۭا); 78:30 `ز ي د` (نَّزِيدَكُمْ); 78:17 `و ق ت` (مِيقَٰتًۭا).
- Synthesis: The ledger also supports a fiscal scene in which liability has amount, document, collector, and deadline. Increase appears here as contested valuation or accumulating obligation rather than biological growth.

### 17. P2 (78:17-30): Signs Refused and Appearances That Fail
- Semantic invariant: Evidence is offered through visible signs and inquiry, but denial substitutes false attribution and unstable appearances for durable recognition.
- Surface relation: direct; 78:28 states that the signs were denied, while 78:20 has already presented the unstable visibility of mirage.
- Surprising reach: Falsehood is tested by a charge that loses nerve, milk that fails to continue, an animal that stops and looks back, a deceptive textile, a disfigured face, and water that never existed.

#### Subchannel A. Visible Sign, Directed Attention, and Investigative Recognition
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An observer identifies a marked target, investigates it, and uses mental steadiness and opened insight to recognize what the sign indicates.
- Active motifs: intentionally seeking a person’s visible mark (`quranic:root_000074:B002/m01`); manifest sign or identifying person (`quranic:root_000074:B003/m01`); investigative inquiry for news (`quranic:root_000318:B010/m01`); steadiness and intelligence (`quranic:root_000332:B003/m01`); insight that opens a closed problem (`quranic:root_001124:B008/m01`); known object available to report (`quranic:root_000831:B001/m01`); long-distance sight (`quranic:root_000832:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 78:27 `ح س ب` (حِسَابًۭا); 78:28 `ء ي ي` (بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا); 78:29 `ح ص ي` (أَحْصَيْنَٰهُ), `ش ي ء` (شَىْءٍ); 78:19 `ف ت ح` (فُتِحَتِ).
- Synthesis: A sign requires an act of directed recognition: the observer seeks its identifying form, investigates, and opens the problem it poses. Counting intelligence and long sight make refusal a failure of attention rather than lack of available evidence.

#### Subchannel B. Denial, False Attribution, and the Lying Self
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A true claim is rejected by labeling it false, while the denying subject generates a contrary account from an unreliable self.
